# Additive screening of 2026-10-18, scenario 8

## Run identity

```yaml
model: claude-sonnet-5
served_version: claude-sonnet-5
tree: sha256:7d84079327a6496ad99d1047ec2c9d0f82a810c3765b0ae22a4ae3ea97be60e8
lock: sha256:1fb804541b7441a6588f57cc93c893118896dbf705119c83b1f9f77072386124
selection: sha256:0e2113cd81af83407d78a1964326c730f04457f9a81c929f9e32bfc73585bda3
read_set: sha256:30284342a7371374ee5ded1720314683046f3f9f7b372ecbb70ff7e6f3e8b5d3
seed: 0

arm: scenario-8

```

**This line is one of the screening round of the additive campaign that [#1775](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/issues/1775) asked for.** `tools/probe/campaign.sh` recorded it as line 9 of the plan of the batch, a plan that draws its 15 lines from `tools/probe/additive-screening.spec`. It is the `scenario-8` arm of the additive tier over 4 probes of the tuning set, at 18 repetitions per probe, on `claude-sonnet-5` under Claude Code 2.1.288 with a cap of 80 turns for each session.

**One tree, one pin, one attempt.** Every session ran in a fresh copy of a `git archive` of the pinned tree, and the engine of that tree. The pin was `2d212a26` for the whole batch, and the batch did not restart. The `tree` digest in the identity above is the digest the assembly recorded.

**What the recorder counted.** The line holds 72 sessions and cost 3045 cents: the intent hook live in 0, 0 stopped at the turn cap, 0 stopped at the session budget, 6 paths outside the workspace named, 0 sessions uncounted, 0 web-tool calls. A session the recorder could not count adds nothing to either count, so each count is a floor.

**What the halts spent.** The usage limit of the account halted the batch more than once. Before a halt stopped it, an attempt at 2 of its sessions spent 67 cents, and the driver then ran each of those sessions again from the start. The cost above counts only the attempt that recorded, so this line spent 67 cents more than it states, and the batch total over every attempt is in [#1775](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/issues/1775).

**The contract of this batch.** The sessions ran under the recorder contract of spec 15: a `bwrap` sandbox with no network of its own, a configuration directory that holds a copy of the credentials and nothing else, the permission mode `dontAsk`, and `WebSearch` and `WebFetch` denied. The round approved $700 over every attempt, and the driver ran with a cap of 70000 cents and a session budget of 300 cents.

**Where the raw recordings are.** The raw session logs are outside git, under `/mnt/build/hw-1775/screening/a/sessions/L9-*/raw.jsonl` on the host that ran the batch. Nobody deletes them until the report of [#1779](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/issues/1779) is published.

**Where the figures are.** The rates, the intervals and the comparisons of this line are in the generated result that reads this transcript, and this document states none of them.

      argument: "{\"description\":\"Research rule declaration and check implementation pattern\",\"subagent_type\":\"Explore\",\"prompt\":\"I'm working in a repository called Headwater, a documentation/taxonomy governance system with a Rust engine (under engine/) and a taxonomy declared somewhere under taxonomy-source/ or .headwater/. Where existing rules/checks are DECLARED (e.g., the taxonomy YAML/source files that declare rule identifiers, names, severities like \\\"error\\\"/\\\"advisory\\\", and categories). Where rules are IMPLEMENTED in the Rust engine (under engine/). Find the source file(s) implementing checks (e.g. search for \\\"retired_term\\\", \\\"hard_wrap\\\", \\\"line_break\\\", \\\"american_spelling\\\" in engine/ Rust code). Show the function/module structure — how a single check is registered and what the severity enum/type looks like (e.g. Error vs Advisory vs Pending).\\n3. Whether there's a concept of \\\"summary\\\" already in the taxonomy's document frontmatter/metadata model — i.e. do documents have a \\\"summary\\\" field declared somewhere in taxonomy.yml or similar? Find where document frontmatter fields are declared (kind, facets, etc.) and check if \\\"summary\\\" exists as a field.\\n4. Find the identifier scheme for rules — e.g. is each rule given an ID like a taxonomy rule ID? Check .headwater/taxonomy.lock or docs/taxonomies/ for a list of rule IDs/names, to see naming convention.\\n5. Look at docs/spec/ (particularly anything about \\\"check layer\\\" i.e. HW-SPEC-check-layer) for documentation of how rules are specified, and docs/taxonomies/ for the taxonomy rule catalog doc if any.\\n\\nReport back: exact file paths and line numbers/snippets for (a) the declaration site pattern for a rule with its severity, (b) the implementation site pattern in Rust, (c) whether \\\"summary\\\" field exists in frontmatter schema and where, (d) the rule ID/naming convention. Keep the report focused and include concrete file paths. Under 500 words plus code snippets.\"}"

    - tool: "Agent"
      argument: "{\"description\":\"Find decision record chain about terminal session number\",\"subagent_type\":\"Explore\",\"prompt\":\"In this repository (/mnt/build/hw-1775/screening/a/ws/L9-additive-scenario-8-p3-r12), I need to trace a chain of decision records (files matching docs/decisions/*.md or similarly named HW-DR-NNNN records).\\n\\nI'm looking for a decision record that is \\\"in force today\\\" (i.e., not superseded) which concerns: the project's front page showing a recorded terminal session, where that session displays a number that no live run currently produces — and this is only valid \\\"if a date marker stands beside it\\\" (some kind of staleness/freshness marker requirement).\\n\\nThat record replaced an earlier decision record (i.e., superseded an earlier one).
